# Implement the GitHub issue watcher (`superclaude issue-watch`) with fakes and PATH stubs only

## Task Overview

Issue 331 on IronbellyOrg/IronClaude asks for a watcher that polls GitHub every 5 minutes for issues that are tagged, assigned exactly to the own login, approved by an allow-listed human, unchanged since approval and in the Project Backlog column, and for at most one such issue per cycle starts `/sc:issue --claim <N>` in the owner's workspace through `orca agent launch`. The owner chose option A (a workspace-side poller), claim-only scope and an integer-only prompt (D1 to D3). This task builds the repo-side part that can be tested with mocks. The driving design is `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md`; where the design and the research disagree, `research/06-gap-fill-decisions.md` (decisions DF-01 to DF-60) wins. Twenty-nine of those decisions depart from literal design or spec text; they are listed under Open Questions in the Task Log and are carried into the fidelity-gate prompts so that a listed departure is not reported as drift.

The deliverables are: the `issue_watch` package (eight modules plus an empty `__init__.py`: `runner`, `github`, `predicates`, `record`, `launch`, `audit`, `cycle`, `cli`) behind a Click group with five commands (`once`, `status`, `check-config`, `init-state`, `reset`; six usage forms because `reset` has two); its two-line registration in the CLI registry (`main.py`); the suite under `tests/issue_watch/` (an empty package marker, `conftest.py`, 8 per-module test files, 11 cross-cutting test files and 13 `_assumed_shape` JSON fixtures; stubs for `gh`, `orca`, `orca-ide` and `git` are written at test time into `tmp_path`, nothing executable is committed); and the owner runbook `docs/designs/issue-watcher-setup.md`. Nothing is deployed, installed, scheduled or run live: no real `gh`, `orca`, `git` or network call occurs in any test, and the task itself runs no `gh label create`. The work is split into ten phases (P1 to P10) with four intermediate six-agent QA gates (after P3, P5, P7 and P8), a seven-agent final gate and a three-agent source-fidelity gate in P9 (34 lens agents in all; the second fix cycle of each of the six gates is written out as five compact conditional items that complete as skipped unless the first cycle fails, 209 checklist items in total), one local checkpoint commit in P10, and the POST reflect gate as the penultimate item followed only by the Done item, which also stages the task folder.

## Key Objectives

The following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:

1. **The package:** `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/` holds `__init__.py`, `runner.py`, `github.py`, `predicates.py`, `record.py`, `launch.py`, `audit.py`, `cycle.py` and `cli.py`, with the intra-package import rules of design section 5.3, Python 3.10 compatibility, and every decision DF-01 to DF-60 applied.
2. **The registration:** `src/superclaude/cli/main.py` gains exactly the import line and the `main.add_command(issue_watch_group, name="issue-watch")` line after the `reflect` registration (decision DF-29), and `uv run superclaude issue-watch --help` lists exactly the five commands.
3. **The tests:** `tests/issue_watch/` passes under `uv run pytest tests/issue_watch/ -v` and under xdist, adds no pytest marker and no dependency, writes only under `tmp_path`, and covers AC-1 to AC-25 including crash points C1 to C5 with five reruns each.
4. **The runbook:** `docs/designs/issue-watcher-setup.md` documents runbook steps 1 to 10 of the spec for the owner, states no unverified gh or Orca shape as fact, and contains no retired doc path and no model-alias string.
5. **The gates:** every command of research/06 Table 1.2 (G-1 to G-11) passes and is recorded, and all QA gates pass with serialized fixes, within two fix cycles per gate.
6. **The checkpoint and the POST reflect:** exactly one local commit (no push, no PR), a POST reflect run over `<BASE SHA>..HEAD`, and the Done item last, all in P10.

### Source Areas

- The `issue_watch` package inside the CLI package tree (nine files, all created by this task).
- The CLI registry module `main.py` (edited: two registration lines).
- The `tests/issue_watch` suite (all created by this task) and the existing `tests/conftest.py` autouse fixtures it must coexist with.
- The owner runbook under `docs/designs` (created by this task).
- Reused or mirrored existing code, read-only: the swarm `atomic` helper (`write_prepared_bytes`), the `install_user_setup` Orca probe (`_ORCA_CLIS`, `_find_orca`), the `sc-issue-protocol` skill (`origin_repo.py`, `refs/claim.md`, `templates/claim-comment.md`).
- Repository gates: `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`.

### Key Constraints

- UV only for every Python operation (`uv run pytest`, `uv run python`, `uvx --offline ruff@0.15.20`); never `python -m`, bare `pip` or bare `pytest`; no new dependency, no new pytest marker, no `hypothesis`, Python 3.10 floor, tests safe under xdist and writing only under `tmp_path`.
- Every `gh` call names `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude` (or the literal repository inside a GraphQL constant); never `SuperClaude-Org`, never an `{owner}` placeholder; only a validated integer (`type(v) is int`, 1 to 999999999) reaches the launch prompt `/sc:issue --claim <n>`; the poller never writes to GitHub.
- Nothing live: no real `gh`, `orca`, `orca-ide`, `git` or network call in any test, no cron entry, no `gh label create`, no push, no PR; never stage or commit anything under `.claude/` (and never `git add -A` or `--no-verify`); `.dev/tasks` is always staged; the P10 checkpoint commit item HALTs on any commit or hook failure (stop rule) and nothing inside this task retries or works around it.

### Handoff File Convention

This task uses intra-task handoff patterns. Items write intermediate outputs to:
**`.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RF-issue-watcher-20261107-150955/phase-outputs/`**

Subdirectories:
- `discovery/` - baselines, tool versions, citation anchors
- `test-results/` - raw pytest, lint and gate command output with structured summaries
- `reviews/` - gate verdicts and reviews
- `plans/` - conditional-action outputs (BASE SHA, gate verdicts, commit allowlist check)
- `reports/` - gate change-set aggregations, the AC trace, the deviation register, post-completion validation

QA agents write to `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-RF-issue-watcher-20261107-150955/qa/` (subfolders `gate-a/`, `gate-b/`, `gate-c/`, `gate-d/`, `final/`, `m4/`). These files persist across all batches and session rollovers. Later items read them by path.

no free function `run_command` is created); and `SubprocessRunner.run` which raises `TypeError` when `argv` is a `str` or is not a list of `str`, otherwise calls `subprocess.run(argv, shell=False, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, cwd=cwd)` with the literal keyword `shell=False`, truncates stderr by BYTES (encode to UTF-8, cut at 500, decode with `errors="ignore"`: a character cut by the byte limit is dropped, so the re-encoded text never exceeds 500 bytes; `errors="replace"` would add a 3-byte U+FFFD and is wrong here), maps `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` to `CommandResult(returncode=None, timed_out=True, ...)` (partial output may be bytes or None: decode safely), maps `OSError` (including `FileNotFoundError` and `PermissionError`) to `spawn_error=type(exc).__name__` with empty output, and measures `duration_s` with `time.monotonic()` around the child only (the one clock read outside the cycle; it is never used for a decision). <!-- evidence-absence: this item creates a new file; the design anchors are docs/designs/issue-watcher.md:218 and :252-265 --> The module must contain no JSON parsing and no knowledge of `gh`; (Gate A covers Phases 2 and 3 together, placed after Phase 3 per template I15 and M2; research/06 Table 1.6. it never imports `subprocess`; it never calls `subprocess` directly; no `fcntl` or `/proc` access at import time) with this first part: the module constants listed here, which carry the values of design section 14 (the name `THRESHOLDS` is in `superclaude.contracts.__all__` and must not be bound): `MAX_CANDIDATES = 10`, `MAX_LAUNCHES_PER_CYCLE = 1`, `DAY_CAP = 6`, `GH_TIMEOUT_S = 20.0`, `PROBE_TIMEOUT_S = 10.0`, `GIT_TIMEOUT_S = 10.0`, `LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_S = 120.0`, `DEADLINE_TOTAL_S = 240.0`, `GH_FLOOR_S = 5.0`, `LAUNCH_CUTOFF_S = 120.0`, `SKEW_WARN_S = 60`, `HEALTH_AGE_S = 900`, `STALE_AGE_S = 86400`, `DISCOVERY_WARN = 80` and the filesystem deny set `{"nfs", "nfs4", "cifs", "smb3", "sshfs", "fuse.sshfs", "9p"}`; `ConfigError(reason, detail)`; a frozen `Config` (`enabled`, `trigger_label`, `approval_label`, `project_id`, `owner_account_id`, `worktree`, `approvers_file`, `state_dir`); `load_config(path) -> Config` implementing the table of design lines 127-139 (JSON only; `schema_version` an int equal to 1; `enabled` a bool defaulting to false; `labels.trigger` and `labels.approval` non-empty, at most 50 characters, no control characters, approval different from trigger, and, per decision DF-20, no `,` and no `"`; `project_id` matching `^PVT_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; `owner_account_id` optional, an int and not a bool, 1 to 10**20-1; `worktree`, `approvers_file` and `state_dir` absolute strings; the `state_dir` default is `Path(os.environ.get("XDG_STATE_HOME") or Path.home() / ".local/state") / "superclaude" / "issue-watch"` and must be absolute; a `~` inside a JSON value is NOT expanded and therefore fails the absolute rule, DF-13; unknown keys at the top level and under `labels` are rejected; a missing file or non-JSON text gives `config_invalid`), including the network filesystem refusal shared by every command that loads a config (DF-53): a pure `fs_type_of(path, mountinfo_text) -> str | None` (DF-23: field 5 is the mount point with the octal escapes `\040`, `\011`, `\012`, `\134` decoded; the filesystem type is the first field after the ` - ` separator; the longest mount point that is a path prefix of `os.path.realpath(state_dir)` or of its nearest existing parent wins) and a module-level `_read_mountinfo() -> str | None` (DF-52: reads `/proc/self/mountinfo`, returns None on `OSError`, and is the function the tests monkeypatch) so a deny-listed type gives `ConfigError("config_invalid")` and an unreadable mountinfo skips the check; `check_platform()` raising `ConfigError("platform_unsupported")` unless `sys.platform == "linux"`; `validate_approvers(path) -> frozenset[int]` per design lines 141-149 (`os.lstat` then `stat.S_ISREG`, so a symlink is rejected; `st_uid == os.geteuid()`; `st_mode & (stat.S_IWGRP | stat.S_IWOTH) == 0`; lines blank or starting `#` ignored; every other line must match `^[1-9][0-9]{0,19}$`; at least one id; duplicates collapse; any failure is `ConfigError("approvers_invalid")`) and `approvers_summary(path) -> tuple[str, int]` (mtime as a UTC `Z` string and the entry count, for `check-config`); `validate_setup(config) -> frozenset[int]` (the worktree must be an existing directory else `ConfigError("worktree_invalid")`, then `validate_approvers`); the `Clock` Protocol (`now() -> datetime`, `monotonic() -> float`) and `SystemClock`; `Deadline(clock, total=240.0)` with `remaining()`, `elapsed()`, `timeout_for(configured) -> float | None` (the lesser of the configured value and the time left, or None when fewer than 5 s remain) and `launch_allowed()` (False once more than 120 s have elapsed); the frozen `Deps` dataclass (`runner`, `store`, `launcher`, `clock`, `audit`, `config`, `which`, `host`; a dataclass, not a Protocol, DF-45) and the frozen `CycleResult` (`exit_code`, `reason`, `cycle_id`, `launched_issue`, `would_launch`, `probe`, `lines`); and `resolve_exit(conditions: frozenset[int]) -> int` returning the first present of 2, 6, 7, 5, 3, 4, else 75 only when 75 is the sole condition, else 0 (design line 166, OD-10); and `exit_for(reason: str) -> int` returning `audit.REASON_TABLE[reason][1]` (decision DF-41: one table feeds the pin test, the exit mapping and the kind check, so every exit condition `run_cycle` raises is `exit_for(<reason>)` and never a literal other than 0, and `resolve_exit` is the only place that combines conditions). <!-- evidence-absence: this item creates a new file; the design anchors are docs/designs/issue-watcher.md:224 and :123-166 --> Ensuring `load_config` performs no external call and no write, the filesystem check is testable without a real `/proc`, and the module binds no name from `superclaude.contracts.__all__` and contains no requirement-id regex literal. here `enabled` false and not dry-run gives exit 0 `not_enabled` with a cycle line and zero external calls, `validate_setup` failures give exit 2 `worktree_invalid` / `approvers_invalid` with a cycle line (state_dir is known); (4) `store.load()` (`StoreError` gives exit 6; a migrated store is saved first only when not dry-run, DF-17) and a persisted `next_permitted_at` in the future gives exit 5 `rate_limited` with zero gh calls; (5) `github.user`: timeout gives exit 3 `gh_timeout`, non-JSON `gh_non_json`, auth failure or any other failure `gh_auth_failed`, a rate limit exit 5 with the time from `github.rate_limit_wait` persisted to `next_permitted_at` (not in dry-run), a login not matching `^[A-Za-z0-9](?:[A-Za-z0-9-]{0,38})$` exit 3 `login_invalid` before discovery, a configured `owner_account_id` that differs from `.id` exit 2 `account_id_mismatch` with no other gh call, and a `Date` header more than 60 s from the local clock writes one `clock_skew` warning line and sets `clock_skew_s` (an unparseable header skips the check); (6) `launch.check_origin(deps.runner, worktree, deadline.timeout_for(10))` (the cycle tests the value for `None` first: `Deadline.timeout_for` returns None when fewer than 5 s remain, and a None must never reach `runner.run`, where it would mean no timeout; `check_origin` fails closed on None, so the result is exit 2 `origin_mismatch` and no process is started) with a mismatch giving exit 2 `origin_mismatch`; (7) `record.sweep` (dry-run reports `pending_orphan` and `claim_not_observed` decision lines without saving); (8) `github.discover` (kinds `truncated` and `discovery_duplicate` give exit 4; 80 or more results write a `discovery_count` warning); (9) order with `predicates.eligibility_order` and keep `MAX_CANDIDATES`; (10) per candidate local checks: a `claimed` entry skips `already_dispatched` with no call; a `launched`, `unknown` or `abandoned` entry gets ONLY the marker read and moves to `claimed` (T-10, decision line `claimed`) when the marker is in the window, else skips `already_dispatched`; an `abandoned` entry whose `pending_at` is null (an `attempts_exhausted` tombstone) is treated like a `failed` entry in backoff: the marker and events reads establish the current approval, which is the window floor passed to `record.mark_claimed(..., approval=...)` (design line 492), and if the approval cannot be established it skips `already_dispatched` and never raises (a `marker_in_window` call with neither `pending_at` nor an approval floor must not happen); a `failed` entry still in backoff gets the marker and events reads to establish the current approval floor (T-12) and otherwise skips `in_backoff` (an implementation reading of spec line 103, recorded as Open Question OQ-36 in the Task Log: apply it as written); a candidate with no entry or a `failed` entry past backoff with attempts below 3 first checks the day cap (`day_cap` skip before any per-issue call); (11) GitHub checks in order marker, approval events, edit timestamps, project items, each with its own skip reason (`marker_unreadable`, `events_incomplete` / `events_unreadable`, `not_approved` / `approval_before_trigger` / `approval_before_init`, `edits_unreadable`, `edited_after_approval`, `project_unreadable`, `project_mismatch`, `budget_exhausted` when the next read would breach the 32 ordinary calls), an own marker in the window giving `claimed` with `launches` 0 (T-11, T-12), `scope_missing` giving exit 3 `project_read_failed`, a rate limit giving exit 5 (entries already written and the `considered` stamps of examined candidates stand, FR-028; a per-issue rate limit always clamps to 3600 s with `rate_limit_clamped`, DF-51), and a per-issue read failure skipping only that issue (FR-022); (12) the first fully eligible candidate: in dry-run stop here (no probe, no Orca call) and record a decision line for the issue with the reason `launched` and `dry_run` true, `would_launch` and `probe` "skipped" on the result (Open Question OQ-39: apply it as written); otherwise `launch.find_orca(deps.runner, which=deps.which, timeout=deadline.timeout_for(10))` once per cycle (the cycle first tests `deadline.timeout_for(10)` for `None`: when it is None the probe is not started, a `budget_exhausted` decision line is written for the candidate and no entry is made; none found gives exit 2 `orca_not_found` and no entry), `launch.build_claim_prompt` (a `ValueError` writes `record.fail` `prompt_invalid`), the re-read with the 3 reserved calls (G7, G3', G6'; a closed issue, a lost label, a voided approval or a changed Project status gives `revalidation_failed` with no entry; a re-read whose assignee set is no longer exactly {own login} gives `assigned_other` (`unassigned` for an empty set) through `predicates.is_intended_owner`, with no entry (FR-008, spec line 140); a timeout or 5xx gives `record.fail` `revalidation_transient`; a refused reserved call gives `budget_exhausted` and no entry), `record.day_cap_count` again (`day_cap`), `store.disabled()` again (exit 0 `disabled_mid_cycle` with no entry and no health stamp), `launch.check_origin` again with `deadline.timeout_for(10)` (exit 2, no entry; a None value fails closed as in step 6), the launch cutoff (`deadline.launch_allowed()` false gives a `budget_exhausted` decision line and no entry), then `record.mark_pending(doc, n, ctx, now)` + `store.save` BEFORE `deps.launcher.launch(..., timeout=120)`, where `ctx` is the `record.PendingContext` of Step 4.1a built with exactly its six fields (`approver_id`, `approval_event_id` and `approved_at` from the `ApprovalResult` of the approval check, `launch_cwd` the configured worktree as an absolute string, `launch_login` the own login and `launch_account_id` the account id read by `github.user`; Open Question OQ-48), then the outcome transition + `store.save`; after any launch-step outcome, later candidates are neither read nor stamped (the S1 reading, decision DF-50); in dry-run the shadow `considered` map and shadow launches live in `dry-run.json` and the shadow day cap counts real `consumed_at` values plus shadow launches (an unparseable `dry-run.json` is replaced by an empty shadow and writes one `dry_run_shadow_reset` warning line, DF-22); (13) one final `store.save` (not in dry-run) with the `considered` stamps for every examined candidate except `budget_exhausted`, pruned to the discovery set, `consumed_log` pruned, and a failing save giving exit 6 `state_dir_unwritable` through `audit.stderr_fallback`; (14) the health stamp `last_completed_cycle_at` only on an exit-0 cycle that is not `disabled`, `not_enabled`, `disabled_mid_cycle` or dry-run; (15) audit: `rotate_if_needed` before the first append (lock held), one decision line per examined issue and entry transition, then one cycle line with `exit`, `reason`, `gh_calls`, `duration_ms` (from the clock) and the optional `discovery_count` / `clock_skew_s`, with `audit.stderr_fallback` when the append fails; the exit code is `resolve_exit` of the conditions raised, each condition taken as `exit_for(<reason>)` from the reason table (DF-41; a `failed`, `unknown` or `abandoned` `spawn_error` entry written this cycle raises 7). <!-- evidence-absence: this item appends to the file created in Step 6.1; ### Phase 9 Final Gate: Final M3 lens-based QA of the whole change set (standard intensity, 7 agents)

(The Final gate verifies the FINAL state of everything built in Phases 2 to 9 and runs BEFORE the M4 fidelity gate. Seven report-only agents = 3 `rf-qa` + 3 `rf-qa-qualitative` + 1 domain-lens `rf-qa` for AC-1 to AC-25 coverage (research/06 Table 1.6). One fix agent only on blocking findings; two verification agents; at most 2 fix cycles; the retry-monotonicity halt guards apply; this gate's counters are independent of every other gate's. **D. Implementation readings and proposals made by this task's items (not part of the 29; each is a choice the sources leave open). Two of them depart from literal source text: OQ-36 from spec line 103 and OQ-41a from design line 614; they are authorized readings like the others of this section, are named explicitly in the exemption sentence of the M4 fidelity prompts, and the owner may confirm or reverse them after the task.**

- OQ-36: a `failed` entry still in backoff gets the marker read plus the events read (to establish the current approval for transition T-12) before it skips `in_backoff`; spec line 103 says the marker read is the only per-issue call such an entry gets, but T-12 needs the approval time.
- OQ-37: in `unchanged_since` a timestamp exactly equal to the approval time counts as changed (fail closed); the sources say "before" / "older than".
- OQ-38: a present trigger label with no `labeled` trigger event in the history gives `events_incomplete` (absent evidence never counts as positive).
- OQ-39: in a dry-run, the would-launch decision line uses the reason `launched` with `dry_run` true and no `state` key; the shadow day cap counts real `consumed_at` values plus the shadow launches.
- OQ-40: an Orca reply that mentions `agent_session_operation_unknown` is `unknown` even if it also carries an operation id.
- OQ-41: names and shapes that the design leaves open are proposals made in the item text (for example `github.Read`, `github.Reply`, the `timeout_for` callable, `PendingContext`, `SloNumbers`, `OPERATION_ID_PATHS`, `FileStore.migrated`); tests pin whatever the module items define.
- OQ-41a: the per-cycle Orca probe result is held by the cycle (one local value per `once` process) and not inside the `OrcaLauncher` instance as design line 614 says; the behaviour (one probe at most per cycle, cached) is the same and `Launcher` keeps the single `launch` method of design lines 277-279. `status_report` keeps the `audit` argument that design line 224 fixes although `status` reads nothing from the audit log (design line 726).
- OQ-47: the operation id of an `unknown` Orca launch (Steps 5.1, 5.2, 5.5): the design says only that the id comes "from the error" when a reply is lost (D 636, 687-689) and gives no rule for finding it in an error reply. The task implements a conservative rule: the id is looked up through `OPERATION_ID_PATHS` first in the JSON object parsed from stdout and then in the JSON object parsed from stderr, and must pass the pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$`; plain text that is not a JSON object never yields an id, so `operation_id` stays null and `status` prints no retry command for that entry. The owner-run live test (FU-1) should replace this with a captured error reply.
- OQ-48: the fields of `record.PendingContext` (Steps 4.1a and 6.2): no source (design, spec or research) defines this type; it is a name this task coins. The task defines exactly six fields: `approver_id`, `approval_event_id`, `approved_at`, `launch_cwd`, `launch_login`, `launch_account_id`, the minimal set derived from the Entry fields written at the `pending` write (D 426-433) and transition T-4 (D 481); `consumed_at` and `pending_at` come from `now` and `launches` is set to 1 by `mark_pending`.
- OQ-49: the reason `re-approved` is in the closed reason table (kind `note`, exit 0, used only in a `note` key; spec 330-358, design line 716) and is pinned by the 57-name test of Steps 2.3 and 2.8, but no item of Phases 6 to 8 makes `cycle.py` emit it or tests an emission. This task adds no code for it: the name exists in the table and is never written on a line; the owner may decide after the task whether an approval that is re-given after a void should write the note.
- OQ-50: `status_report` (Step 6.3) builds each `retry[].argv` from the first `launch._ORCA_CLIS` name that `which` resolves. Design line 741 is silent on the case where `which` resolves none of them. The task uses the bare first name `orca-ide` as `argv[0]` in that case and never raises (`status` runs no probe and must still print the stored retry command for the operator); the AC-9 test pins PATH so that it does not depend on the machine (Step 7.4).
- OQ-51: if the owner withdraws the checkpoint commit (OQ-7, OQ-35), Step 10.2 records `commit withdrawn by owner` in the Task Log and in `commit-result.md`, Steps 10.3 and 10.4 are skipped and Step 10.7 accepts that record in place of an exit-0 commit result. The POST reflect then runs over a base the owner names; when the owner names none, it runs with the working-tree diff against HEAD as its change set, which is a weaker change-set definition than `BASE..HEAD` (it includes files that were already modified before this task) and is documented here for the owner.

### Reflect Depth Derivation

Computed at build time (2026-11-07) with the frozen extraction rules of the task-builder skill over this file's body (frontmatter, fenced blocks and the Open Questions section excluded for S1, S2 and S4) and the spec file `docs/designs/issue-watcher.md`: S1 (distinct files) = 227, S2 (distinct subsystems, first two path segments) = 31, S3 (distinct FR and NFR ids in the spec) = 43, S4 (dependency tokens) = 11, S5 (no item Context cites an OQ id, so the fallback counts the non-empty Open Questions entries; 49 entries at build time, 52 after the qualitative-review additions OQ-49 to OQ-51) = 52, S6 (type is "✨ Feature", not a refactor class) = 0. TCS = 3*227 + 4*31 + 2*43 + 2*11 + 5*52 + 4*0 = 1173, far above the threshold 35, so the band is `deep` (Tier 2 forced); the O4 floor (never `quick`) is met. The POST reflect item (Step 10.6) therefore passes `--depth deep`. The count of checklist items at build time was 176; after the task-integrity QA fixes of 2026-11-08 (Step 3.1 and Step 4.1 each split into three parts, thirty compact conditional cycle-2 gate items added, Step 10.8 folded into Step 10.7) it is 209, and the S5 count and the TCS above were refreshed for the 52 Open Questions entries, and the other terms were not recomputed because the band cannot change (1173 against the threshold 35). The pre-execution reflect sign-off block in the frontmatter (`reflect_pre`) is left at its template defaults for the skill's PRE gate to fill; `reflect_post_mode` is `skill` and the frontmatter carries neither `start_commit` nor `executor_model_class`.
